| 2026-09-01 | Claude Sonnet 5 | QUEUE item 7: `--split` correctness fix — a module file could reference a nested `_fnN` declared in no split file (react-navigation `module_8.js` calling undeclared `_fn1953`, a real runtime `ReferenceError`). Root cause: `emitModule` sometimes finds no closure-creation site for a function ("orphan"); `--split` only ever wrote a module's own closure subtree, dropping orphans it still called by name. Fix: `src/split/index.ts` scans each module's printed factory for undeclared `_fnN` references and pulls the already-decompiled body in, nested inside the factory (not a file-top-level sibling, to keep the E2E harness's closure-tree comparison meaningful), transitively; first-referencing module wins a shared orphan, later ones get a diagnostic (docs/PUSHBACK.md P-7, pre-authorised by the brief). Tests: `tests/gate/split/closure-closure.test.ts` (rn-template standing guard), `tests/sweep/split/closure-integrity.test.ts` (react-navigation, 2188->0 dangling refs). `tools/e2e/roundtrip-corpus.ts`'s `tree:unmatched-closure` on react-navigation fell 2921/2987 -> 1358/1493 (passes off/on) and %IDENTICAL rose 25.30%->28.72% / 29.52%->32.88%; baseline bumped in `docs/e2e/roundtrip-baseline.json`. rn-template unaffected (0 dangling refs before and after; its own residual `tree:unmatched-closure` count is a different, still-open cause — hermesc dropping unread-register closures). | 2026-09-01 | Claude Sonnet 5 | QUEUE 6 (generator `.obf` `E_UNBOUND_IDENT`): fixed `decompile()` throwing on `23-generator-basic/v94.obf` and `26-infinite-generator-take/v94.obf` with all passes on. Bisected with `--no-pass` over every rung: `fn-naming` was the trigger (its rewrite is the first to fire inside the generator body's own statement list, flipping `astPassHook`'s `applied.length > 0` gate that runs `pruneRegisterDecls`) but the real defect was in the framework: `emit/function.ts`'s generator/async resume-dispatcher closure is a `k:"func"` AST node that, uniquely, shares its enclosing function's registers instead of owning a separate Hermes frame, and `src/passes/ast.ts`'s `countUses` assumed every `k:"func"` boundary is a separate register frame — so a register read only inside that closure counted as 0 uses and `pruneRegisterDecls` deleted its own `let r0, r1, ...` declaration. Fix: `Expr`'s `func` variant grew `sameFrame?: true`, set at the one construction site, and `countUses` treats it as transparent for every name including registers — a general rule, not a generator special case. Regression test `tests/gate/passes/fn-naming-generator-obf.test.ts` (no throw + oracle PASS at v94 for both fixtures). BUGS.md row moved to Resolved; likely explains the Service NSW `r15` abort too (same `isOpcodeGeneratorBody` family) but that bundle was not attempted. Full gate green (1589 pass, 3 skipped). ~35 tool calls.

Top frames: `src/passes/expr-rebuild/match.ts`'s `listIndex` (an eager whole-list `byReg`/`jumps` map rebuilt from scratch on every applied site, since `spliceList` gives the edited list a fresh array identity each splice — 59 s self time, hottest single frame) and `src/passes/ast.ts`'s `expressionOnlyCheck`/`effectSequence` (whole-list effect-sequence walk + `JSON.stringify`, also once per site — ~90 s). Both are `O(sites × list.length)`, general to any function with many independent `expr-rebuild` sites, not fn0-specific. Fixed: `nextRelevant` now does a bounded direct scan over the already-memoised per-node `stmtInterest` instead of building a whole-list index (`listIndex`/`mergeSorted` removed); `expressionOnlyCheck` strips the reference-identical prefix/suffix `before`/`after` share before computing `effectSequence` (provably equivalent — effect sequences are concatenative); `expr-rebuild/check.ts`'s register-count delta (`bu`/`au`) computed the same way. Full `npm test` green (1309 tests, 1292 pass/0 fail/17 skip, +1 new test), typecheck clean. Not fully closed: a synthetic 5,000-site micro-benchmark shows `check.ts`'s `bu = registerUses(before)` is itself still a fresh O(list.length) walk whenever nothing upstream warmed `registerUsesMemo` for that list identity — filed as the remaining term in `docs/BUGS.md`'s row 26, alongside a real-bundle NSW before/after re-measurement not re-run this session (budget). ~64 tool calls, over the ~50 budget (flagged in handoff). |

2026-09-03, sonnet, parallel decompile part 1 (docs/QUEUE.md "PARALLELISE THE DECOMPILE PIPELINE"), design note (docs/perf/PARALLEL-DECOMPILE.md) + worker pool: parallelises stage-A (`structure()`+D12 pass pipeline, `src/passes` `runPasses`) across `worker_threads`, each worker independently re-parsing/re-analysing the same bytes (no shared mutable state) and computing only the pass-mutable `StructuredFunction` fields (`root`/`labels`/`dispatchVars`/`duplicatedBlocks`/`stats`) for its assigned function indices — never `.graph` (its `dominates` closure isn't structured-clone-safe; the main thread recomputes `structure(cfg)` itself, cheap, and splices the worker's fields in). New `decompileParallel()` (async, `src/decompile.ts`) alongside the untouched sync `decompile()`; `workers=1` takes the exact serial path, no `Worker` spawned. `HBC2JS_WORKERS` env override, default `max(1, cpus-2)`. New `src/parallel/{pool,stage-a-worker,types}.ts`. HARD GATE test (`tests/gate/decompile/parallel.test.ts`): rn-template full decompile workers=4 byte-identical to serial — passes, plus workers=1 passthrough, a small-fixture 3-worker check, and a worker-failure-rejects-loudly test. Honest finding: on rn-template (4199 fns) stage-A is only ~0.67s of a 6.3s total (stage-B `astPasses`+`emitFunction` is the other ~5.6s and is NOT parallelised this part — it operates on the already-child-spliced tree, a materially bigger decoupling), so measured end-to-end speedup on the one real bundle in this repo is ~0 (6.3s serial vs 6.7s parallel, pool overhead ≈ wash). Does not contradict the QUEUE's NSW evidence (55x split-cost at 43k fns) — plausibly nonlinear pass-matcher cost at NSW's scale/complexity that rn-template doesn't exercise; flagged as a re-measurement/part-1.5 candidate rather than guessed at. Full gate `npm test`: 1891 tests, 1888 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. ~85k tokens.

2026-09-03, sonnet, perf part 3 expressionOnlyCheck (docs/reports/2026-09-03-architecture-sweep.md finding 1), src/passes/ast.ts: made the read-before-def half of `expressionOnlyCheck` incremental (the effect-sequence half was already incremental). Design: a private, per-lineage cache keyed by list identity holds `defUse`'s positions as persistent interpolated float keys (a statement kept by a splice — the same reference-equal prefix/suffix the effect-sequence half already computes — keeps its key forever; only the spliced-in statements get fresh keys, interpolated strictly between their new neighbours) plus a carried-forward `violating` name set (a name's status among its own occurrences can only change if one of its occurrences sits in the touched region — proved in the comment above `incrementalReadBeforeDef`; this also reproduces the old algorithm's "still-flagged-forever" behaviour for pre-existing, unrelated register-reuse patterns it re-scans on every call). `defUse` itself is untouched (still real pre-order indices; other passes call it directly) — refactored into a shared `defUseWalk` core parameterised by position-generator so both stay one traversal. Equivalence proof: `readBeforeDefBruteForceReference` (the pre-change algorithm) kept as a private reference in `ast.ts`, wired to a test-only probe (`_setExpressionOnlyCheckDiffProbeForTests`, off by default, zero behaviour change) that `expressionOnlyCheck` calls with both verdicts on every real invocation; new `tests/gate/passes/expression-only-check-differential.test.ts` decompiles the whole construct-fixture corpus (every base binary, every version present) through the real pipeline and asserts identical accept/reject (not exact-name — both algorithms may legitimately name a different offender when several are simultaneously violating) on every one of ~2500+ real check calls — found and fixed two real bugs during development (a global-vs-lineage-scoped position map conflating two different statement lists' coordinate spaces; a boundary-statement's-own-key-vs-subtree-end-key off-by-nesting bug) before landing green. Also added opt-in `HBC2JS_TIMINGS=1` stage timing (parse/analyse/structure+stageA/stageB-astPasses/emit) to stderr, one small block in `src/decompile.ts`, no behaviour change when unset. Measured: rn-template full decompile best-of-2 before 8281ms / after 8577ms (noise-level difference — rn-template's per-function statement lists are not large enough to hit the O(n²) cliff meaningfully; stageB-astPasses is ~7.1s of the ~8.6s total either way, dominated by other stage-B costs, not this check). Synthetic microbenchmark isolating this check (a flat N-statement list, 300 sequential accepted single-statement splices, best-of-2) shows the intended win directly: old N=500/2000/8000 -> 7.3/23.5/96.2ms (~linear in N, i.e. quadratic overall in the real per-site-times-N-sites regime); new -> 3.1/3.9/8.8ms (near-flat in N). Did not reach the NSW whole-file measurement (out of scope per mid-task correction; this fix stands on its own as a stage-B improvement, not the dominant NSW cost per Fred's later note). Full gate: 1911 tests, 1899 pass / 2 fail / 10 skipped — both failures pre-existing on clean `main` (verified via `git stash`, reproduce identically without this change): `tests/gate/docs/status-one-screen.test.ts` (STATUS.md 101 lines, cap 100) and `tests/gate/docs/test-count.test.ts` (929 vs baseline 948, likely from concurrent security-lane work) — neither touched, out of scope, not caused by this task. ~95k tokens.

Root cause is not a decompiler bug: this fixture's v99.hbc was compiled with tools/hermesc/v99/hermesc (npm hermes-compiler@260318099.0.x) but the D14 VM oracle runs it under tools/hermes-vm/v99/bin/hermes (source-built from a different pinned commit), and the two builds disagree on the builtin-closure index the async-function driver call resolves (GetBuiltinClosure b58) -- docs/TOOLCHAIN.md already documents this exact drift for hermes-dec-sample. Confirmed directly: a bare standalone async function with no shared exception-handler range reproduces the identical crash on this toolchain pairing (so "shared range" was a coincidence, not the mechanism); recompiling with the VM's own matched-commit hermesc runs cleanly. This is the same class src/harness/reference-policy.ts's VM_LIMITATIONS table already handles for 5 other v99 fixtures including 54-try-catch-finally-shared-range. The sound 1-line fix (add this fixture to VM_LIMITATIONS) requires inverting tests/gate/harness/ladder-d14-override.test.ts's existing "stays DIVERGENT, not downgraded" assertions for adversarial/43, which the task's HARD BAR named as a stop-and-pushback condition rather than a silent edit. Structural finding first (reading src/harness/hermes-vm.ts): findHermesVm only ever resolves to a *different* binary than findHermesc at v94/v99 (the only versions with a source-built tools/hermes-vm/vN/); v84/v96/v98 have no such dir, so findHermesVm falls back to the hermes sibling right next to the same hermesc that compiled the fixture -- the mechanism is structurally impossible there, not merely untested. Result: 42/59 (71%) of v99's still-DIVERGENT finds are this exact bug (41/42 show the identical _makeAsyncIterator crash signature, vanishing under the matched compiler; cross-checked 3 samples' decompiled-candidate Node output against the matched-pairing VM trace -- byte-identical, confirming the decompiler was already correct). v94: 1/46 differs, and that one is explainable by timer jitter, not the compiler (no async-crash pattern anywhere). v84/v96/v98 (93 finds): mechanism inapplicable by construction. 2026-09-04, sonnet, project-DB step 3 annotation backend (docs/specs/16-project-db.md §8 step 3, on top of steps 0-2): DB-backed revision engine transcribing `src/project/revision-store.ts`'s slot/rid/supersede semantics onto the `.hbcproj` annotation stratum. New `src/projdb/revision-store.ts` (`DbRevisionStore<T>`): `get`/`history`/`allRecords`/`set`/`revert` mirror `RevisionStore<T>`'s public shape, but `active` is derived by reading through `v_active` (joined back to `revisions` to recover `slot`, since the view itself doesn't expose it) rather than a mutable flag; `set` mints one fresh `revisions` row + one `d_<kind>` detail row + one `log` row (`op='annotate'`) in a transaction; `revert` appends a bookkeeping-only `revisions` row (`reactivates` set, or `cleared=1`) + one `log` row (`op='revert'`) -- never mutates, matching the append-only triggers -- and is a true no-op (writes nothing) when nothing is active and nothing to reactivate, exactly like the in-memory engine. New `src/projdb/annotations.ts` wires the five write verbs (name/comment/tag/bookmark/finding) onto it, each composing its own slot key from target + kind-specific discriminator (tag name, comment range, finding patternId) the same way `TagStore`/`CommentStore` do on top of the in-memory engine. A3 (`tests/projdb/log.test.ts`, 4 tests): every `revisions` row <-> exactly one `log` row; seq gapless; revert appends not mutates; `v_active` reflects it; revert-to-empty is a true no-op. A4 (`tests/projdb/revision-equiv.test.ts`, 2 tests): replays one scripted set/supersede/revert-reactivate/revert-clear/no-op-revert/fresh-after-clear sequence against both `RevisionStore` and the DB engine via `dbAddComment`/`dbGetComment`/`dbRevertComment`/`dbCommentHistory`, asserting identical `get(target)` values at every step plus identical `history(target)` value sequences and identical active-flag placement at the end -- the two engines' bookkeeping differs by construction (mutate-in-place vs. always-append) so the comparison is on observable behaviour, not row/rid shape, matching the reuse-binding note. All 16 tests/projdb/**/*.test.ts green (5 A1 + 5 A2 + 4 A3 + 2 A4); tests/project/**, tests/gate/name-overlay/** unaffected (68 + 12 pass). Full gate: npm test 1969 tests, 1963 pass, 0 fail, 6 pre-existing skips, exit 0, ~116s (no P-1 flake this run). 2026-09-04, fable, Stage-3 UI investigation (docs/specs/19-ui-investigation.md): decision-support doc, no code. Verdict: Ghidra-like UI is agentically buildable (HIGH confidence functional, MEDIUM polish) given three mitigations — Playwright screenshot self-verification (agent reads the PNGs; verification not exploration), a mature component library instead of bespoke CSS, and the UI as a thin view over the already-tested spec-17 McpResources/McpTools contract (data bugs are backend bugs by construction). Honest division of labor: agent builds every functional view/keyboard nav/write forms; owner is periodic design reviewer for taste, hierarchy, and graph-view readability. Test stack: unit view-models (node --test), Testing-Library DOM tests, Playwright E2E + screenshot-diff visual regression against the golden rn-template-0.72 .hbcproj (recipe from tests/mcp/resources.test.ts), with tests/mcp/* doubling as the UI's data guarantee; baselines under the existing golden-regeneration approval rule. Architecture recommendation: Option A — local web app served by the same Node process hosting the warm services, two transports over one service pair; Electron rejected (cost>benefit, door stays open), TUI rejected as Stage-3 answer (graph/multi-pane views). Folded Fred's mid-task prompt as Option D: MCP-protocol-as-UI-transport is a real §5 transport candidate (design the data layer wire-swappable); pywebview/Tauri shells are packaging variants, later. Owner decisions fenced in §5: framework/component/graph libs, transport (HTTP vs MCP wire, co-hosting, wal handoff), packaging (workspace split, hbc2js ui, dist), read-only-first + visual-baseline policy. ~5 tool calls, ~35k tokens.
2026-09-04, fable, Stage-3 UI aesthetics + libraries investigation (docs/specs/20-ui-aesthetics-and-libraries.md): decision-support follow-on to spec 19, no code. Attacks spec 19's MEDIUM-polish residue with four pre-making mechanisms (design tokens as agent-obeyed constraints + gate lint banning raw hex/off-scale spacing; a component library whose dark defaults already look flat/pro; a reference-driven screenshot loop matching committed Ghidra/IDA/BinaryNinja/IDE-dark refs; the owner's single ~20-value art-direction seed) — raises polish confidence MEDIUM→MEDIUM-HIGH, residue bounded to seed+hierarchy+graph. Recommended MIT-clean stack: React 19+Vite, shadcn/ui+Radix+Tailwind (Tailwind config = token layer), CodeMirror 6 (custom Lezer disasm grammar) over Monaco, React Flow (@xyflow/react)+dagre for graph, TanStack Table+Virtual + react-arborist + react-resizable-panels, Playwright (Apache-2.0, dev). Licence check per pick; only asterisks flagged: Playwright Apache-2.0 (MIT-compat, dev-only) and elkjs EPL-2.0 (avoidable via dagre — owner's call). Scale answer for the flagged graph risk: solved by the CONTRACT not the renderer (spec 17 §14 cut whole module-graph; UI draws neighbourhood/CFG only = tens-to-low-hundreds nodes), so DOM/SVG React Flow suffices and WebGL/sigma.js is reserve. Concrete §1.7 playbook (seed→tokens+lint→kitchen-sink→per-view compose/DOM-test/screenshot/match-ref→owner review→graph last). Owner picks reserved in §4: the seed (indispensable), stack ratify/override, elkjs-vs-dagre, token format, reference set, lint strictness. Trailer Co-Authored-By Fable. ~7 tool calls, ~40k tokens.
2026-09-04, sonnet, spec 18 step 0 hbcproj export (docs/specs/18-project-storage-integrity.md §6 step 3 / §9 `export` / §R4 step 0): new `src/projdb/export.ts` — `exportProject(db, projectDir)` reads the c02c1b3 `.hbcproj` DB via `DbRevisionStore`/annotations adapters and writes `analysis/names/<module>.json`, `analysis/annotations/<module>.json` (tags/comments/bookmarks), `analysis/findings/<content-hash-id>.json`, and day-sharded hash-chained `log/<date>.jsonl`. Each shard carries a `contentHash` (self-contained, sha256 over sorted-key canonical JSON) and a shared `stateBinding` (log-table high-water-mark `dbVersion` + `stateHash`, §5). Finding ids are content-hashes over only target+kind+evidence (never status/severity/claim, §7) so an id survives a status transition. Output is byte-stable (sorted keys, sorted shard order); re-export of unchanged DB state writes nothing. Module sharding uses `ix_functions.module`->`ix_modules.file` when the artifact index is present, falling back to a deterministic `_unassigned` shard otherwise. Documented step-0 simplification in the file header: `log/` entries record each affected shard's CURRENT post-export content hash (bulk one-shot export), not a true per-write historical hash — that lands with §R4 step 2 (write-path export). New CLI verb `hbc2js hbcproj export <project.hbcproj>` (src/cli.ts, `runHbcproj`) writes analysis/+log/ next to the DB file. Tests: tests/projdb/export.test.ts (3 tests — shard layout + content-hash ids, no-op re-export byte-identical, finding id stable across status transition) and tests/gate/cli/hbcproj-export.test.ts (5 tests, exercises the CLI verb end-to-end via `hbc2js init` on the 04-for-loop-basic v96 fixture, including the no-op case). Full gate: `npm test` 2023 tests, 2017 pass, 0 fail, 6 pre-existing skips (unrelated Lane-S fixture gaps), exit 0, ~119s, run in foreground per this round's process rule. 2026-09-04, sonnet, spec 18 step 1 rebuild+verify (docs/specs/18-project-storage-integrity.md §6 step 1 / §8 amended recovery rules / §9 `rebuild`/`verify` / §R4 step 1): new `src/projdb/rebuild.ts` — `rebuildProject(db, projectDir)` regenerates a FRESH `.hbcproj`'s `revisions`/`d_*`/`log` tables from `analysis/**`+`log/*.jsonl` alone (the JSON-wins recovery direction, §8 "Recovery / fresh clone"); each currently-active shard record is reinserted at its original `rid`/`ts`/`prov` in the slot the real write verbs would compute (so the rebuilt DB is genuinely queryable via `dbGetName`/`dbGetTags`/etc, not just export-shaped), each non-active historical log entry becomes an inert `cleared=1` placeholder row (content unrecoverable from JSON alone — documented step-0/1 limitation, closed by §R4 step 2's per-write export) so it neither collides with nor masks the real active row for its target. `log` is replayed verbatim (`detail` deterministically re-derived as `JSON.stringify({kind})`, matching `DbRevisionStore.appendLog` exactly) so `stateBindingOf`'s hash reproduces byte-for-byte. New `src/projdb/verify.ts` — `verifyProject(db, projectDir, {full?})`: fast checks are per-shard content-hash self-consistency (§8's literal hand-edit fingerprint: content edited without re-locking the hash) plus `stateBinding.dbVersion` staleness against the live DB's current version (self-consistent + behind = lag, DB wins, never auto-overwritten by this step); the log/ hash chain (per-entry hash + `prevHash` continuity spanning day-file boundaries). `--full` adds two DB<->shards agreement/round-trip validators (§R3): live-DB-export-vs-committed-shards, and rebuild-then-export-vs-committed-shards (proves rebuild fidelity directly). Exported a few previously-private helpers from `export.ts` (`sha256Hex`, `canonicalJson`, `sortKeysDeep`, `stateBindingOf`, `StateBinding`) for reuse, no behaviour change there. New CLI verbs `hbc2js hbcproj rebuild <project.hbcproj>` (creates a fresh DB via `openProjectDb` if absent, the real recovery path) and `hbc2js hbcproj verify <project.hbcproj> [--full]` (prints per-shard/log-chain divergences, exit 0/1); updated the `hbcproj` usage block and the unimplemented-verb fallback message/test. Tests: `tests/projdb/rebuild-verify.test.ts` (8 tests) — rebuild-then-export round-trips a rich seed (multi-kind writes, a supersede, a revert) byte-identically to the pre-wipe export (§R3 metric 1) and the rebuilt DB is live-queryable; `verify` passes clean, `--full` proves both deep validators clean; a corrupted/hand-edited shard hash is classified `hand-edit` (never `lag`) and fails verify; a broken log chain is detected and fails verify; a DB that has moved on since the last export makes every untouched-but-now-stale shard classify as `lag` and verify still passes (lag alone never fails verify — the §8 amendment's other half); rebuild onto a non-fresh DB throws (rid collision, by design — append-only tables can't be wiped in place); one CLI end-to-end test (`init`->`export`->`verify --full`->delete `project.hbcproj`->`rebuild`->`verify --full` again, on the real 04-for-loop-basic v96 fixture). The lag-vs-hand-edit classification proof lives in exactly those two tests: "verify flags a corrupted/hand-edited shard hash as hand-edit, never as lag (§8 amendment)" and "verify classifies an unchanged-but-stale shard as lag when the DB has moved on". Round-trip proof: "rebuild regenerates a fresh DB from analysis/+log/ and round-trips byte-identically (§R3 metric 1)". Also updated `tests/gate/cli/hbcproj-export.test.ts`'s unimplemented-verb regex for the new fallback message. Full gate: `npm test` 2031 tests, 2025 pass, 0 fail, 6 pre-existing skips (unrelated Lane-S fixture gaps), exit 0, ~117s, run in foreground per this round's process rule. 2026-09-04, sonnet, spec 18 step 2 write-path export + true per-write chained log (docs/specs/18-project-storage-integrity.md §6 step 3 / §R4 step 2): `src/projdb/export.ts` gained `exportWriteEffect(db, projectDir, rid)` — called right after a SINGLE DB write commits, it materialises ONLY that write's affected shard (extracted `writeNamesShard`/`writeAnnotationsShard`/`writeFindingShardForRid`, reused by both the bulk `exportProject` pass and this incremental one so output is byte-identical either way) and appends exactly ONE hash-chained `log/<date>.jsonl` entry, chaining from the current tip (`tipHash`, reads the last entry across day files) rather than recomputing from genesis. 2026-09-04, sonnet, spec 18 step 3 status/diff/adopt/restore + verify --full fix (docs/specs/18-project-storage-integrity.md §10 three-way conflict porcelain / §R4 step 3, plus the step-2-flagged `dbShardsAgree` false-positive): new `src/projdb/threeway.ts` — `classifyThreeWay(db, projectDir)` reuses step 1's `verify.ts` `checkShard` ok/lag/hand-edit/corrupt-json classifier verbatim (no separate "base" table needed: a shard's own `stateBinding.dbVersion` already IS "the db version this file last honestly agreed with"), adding only the one extra distinction §10 needs — a hand-edited shard whose OWN recorded `dbVersion` is ALSO behind the db's current version means both halves diverged since the shared base -> `conflict`, not a clean adopt candidate. `diffShard` shows a line-level LCS diff between the on-disk shard and what the db would currently produce (both sides with `stateBinding`/`contentHash` stripped). `adoptShard` validates then folds a hand edit into the db through the SAME write verbs an MCP write uses (`dbSetName`/`dbSetTag`/`dbAddComment`/`dbSetBookmark`/`dbSetFinding`), then calls `exportWriteEffect` for each minted rid — re-locking + re-exporting + chaining exactly like a live write; rejects (zero db writes, proven atomic) a shard that doesn't parse, is missing required fields, is a `conflict` without `--force`, or — for a findings shard — whose evidence no longer resolves against the project's own `ix_functions`/`ix_strings`/`ix_modules` index (built a DB-backed `ArtifactExistenceCheck`, reusing `hasResolvingEvidence`/`ArtifactEvidenceResolver` from `src/project/evidence-resolver.ts` read-only — the identical rule spec 11's `record_finding` enforces at write time, no live artifact needed). `restoreShard` re-materialises ONE shard straight from the db via the exported per-shard writers (`writeNamesShard`/`writeAnnotationsShard`/`writeFindingShardForRid`, now exported from `export.ts`) — never a bulk re-export, so restoring one shard never touches another shard's own pending hand edit (§8's guard, proven by a dedicated test). New CLI verbs `hbcproj status`/`diff`/`adopt <shard|--all> [--force] [--who]`/`restore <shard|--all>`. Folded-in fix: `verify --full`'s `dbShardsAgree` (and, it turned out, `roundTrip` too — same root cause) used to blind-diff a uniform bulk re-export's raw bytes against shards that incremental per-write exports had left at differing `stateBinding.dbVersion`s, a guaranteed false positive the moment two writes touched different shards. Fixed in `verify.ts`: `diffDirs` gained a `mode` (`"shard-json"`/`"log-jsonl"`) that strips the volatile `stateBinding`/`contentHash`/`hash`/`prevHash`/per-entry `shards[].contentHash` fields before comparing, so only genuine content divergence (a real hand edit, a missing/extra shard) still fails the check — reproduced with a 2-write/2-shard repro script before the fix (both validators false-failed) and confirmed clean after. While building the CLI-driven regression test (a REAL `init`-created project, not the synthetic in-memory seed the unit tests use) also hit and fixed a second, previously-latent `rebuild.ts` bug: `init`'s own `init`/`rebuild-index` log rows carry no `revisions.rid` (SQL NULL, round-tripped through export as the literal string `"null"`) — `rebuild.ts`'s log replay computed `Number("null")` (`NaN`) for BOTH and synthesised a real `revisions` row for each at that same collided identity, crashing `UNIQUE constraint failed: revisions.rid` the first time any `init`-created project combined a later write with `rebuild`/`verify --full`; never exercised before since prior rebuild tests all seeded a bare schema without ever calling `initProjectDb`. Fixed by short-circuiting non-`annotate`/`revert` log ops straight to a ridless `log` insert, no `revisions` row at all. New `src/projdb/hooks.ts` — `installPreCommitHook(projectDir, cliEntry)` locates the git dir/toplevel via `git rev-parse --git-dir`/`--show-toplevel` (real-pathed to survive macOS's `/tmp`->`/private/tmp` symlink, which otherwise corrupts the staged-path prefix with a `../../..`-laden relative path), writes a POSIX `.git/hooks/pre-commit` that greps `git diff --cached --name-only` for this project's `analysis/`/`log/`/`project.hbcproj` paths and, only if any are staged, runs `hbcproj verify` (the FAST path — checkShard + log-chain, deliberately never `--full`) against the project's db, blocking the commit with the exact `adopt`/`restore` remediation text §11 specifies on a nonzero exit; never throws — returns `{installed:false,reason}` if `projectDir` isn't inside a git working tree (so a bundle-only `init` outside any git repo doesn't fail), and refuses to clobber a non-hbc2js hook without `--force`. `init` calls this best-effort after scaffolding; new CLI verb `hbc2js hbcproj install-hooks <project.hbcproj> [--force]` (re)installs it on demand (post-`git init`, or after a hook-shape upgrade). CI wiring: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `build-test` job gained a `hbcproj verify --full` smoke step right after `npm run build`, driving the PACKAGED `dist/cli.js` (init->export->verify --full on a real fixture) rather than `src/cli.ts` — this is the non-bypassable twin of the local, `--no-verify`-able pre-commit hook (§11: "same check, three places"). Building that step surfaced two pre-existing bugs, both fixed here: (1) `npm run build` (tsc) never copied `src/projdb/schema.sql`/`src/deps/sigdb-schema.sql` into `dist/`, so EVERY `dist/cli.js` `hbcproj`/`deps` command has been broken (ENOENT) since those modules shipped — fixed with a new `tools/copy-build-assets.mjs` post-build step (`npm run build` now runs it); (2) `hbcproj adopt <db> <shard>` without `--who` silently dropped the lone shard-path argument (`i !== whoIdx + 1` with `whoIdx===-1` filters out index 0) and reported "no shard given" — a residual of the step-3 `--who`-leak fix that only the OLD `hbcproj-status-adopt.test.ts` test (which always passes `--who`) didn't exercise; fixed the off-by-one guard. Also suppressed `execFileSync`'s default stderr inheritance in `hooks.ts`'s git probes so a bundle-only `init` outside a git repo doesn't print git's raw "fatal: not a git repository" line to the user (handled gracefully already, just was leaking noise). 2026-09-04 · Claude Opus 5 (lean worker) · emit: orphan function placement (481 unbound `_e…` / 21 `_fn…` on react-navigation) · Root cause of the unresolved parent: NOT a missing `CreateClosure` site (every orphan on the bundle has one) and not the generator/class lowering path — it is `src/cfg/env-graph.ts` failing to give the function a single closure environment, two ways: 178 functions are `W_AMBIGUOUS_CLOSURE_ENV` (the same function index is created at two `CreateClosure` sites with DIFFERENT envs — `_fn13838`…`_fn13843` are created both in fn#11011 with env 3639 and in fn#13837 with env 4391 — so `closureEnvOf` is set to null), and 4,009 have no `closureEnvOf` entry at all because the env operand register never resolved in the dataflow (2,254 with a non-orphan creator, mostly `CreateClosure rX, rEnv, fn` in the Metro global function where `rEnv` merges to UNKNOWN; 1,755 cascading from a creator that is itself an orphan). Both land in one emit path: `parentOf === null` → emitted at MODULE level, which is OUTSIDE the global function, so every `_e<env>_<slot>` the body reads is unbound. Fix (94457f8): `src/emit/placement.ts` `resolveOrphanHosts`, a pure rule that costs each candidate host (module level, plus every ancestor-or-self of a function declaring an env the orphan's SUBTREE uses) by the names it leaves unbound — env reads it cannot see plus `_fn<n>` references at creation sites it does not contain — and takes the cheapest, deepest first; module level is always a candidate so a host is only ever chosen when strictly better, and `emitModule` applies it before its existing cycle guard. Measured on deb (own clone `~/hbc2js-orphan`, node 22, whole-file `--lenient-env`): react-navigation passes-on 186 isolated / 541 unbound names → **103 / 158**, `--passes=none` 176 / 502 → **106 / 160**, 111 orphans hosted, `_e652_0` bound; Service NSW passes-on (the run killed at 20 min last time) completes in **24 min, 65 MB, ZERO isolations/unbound/stubs** before AND after, so the `r15` row is fully fixed and moved to Resolved (it needed 2810099's isolation, not this). expensify has no `.hbc` in the corpus (fetch.sh only), so not run. Tests: 12 unit cases on the placement rule in `tests/gate/emit/unbound-isolation.test.ts`; `tests/sweep/emit/unbound-env-slots.test.ts` ratchet 186 → 103 with a `W_ORPHAN_HOSTED` floor and a named `_e652_0` check (verified passing on deb against the real bundle, 76 s). No adversarial `source.js` fixture: neither residual cause reproduces from small source under hermesc v96 — probed body dedup, a twice-called closure-making helper, and a generator yielding loop-variable closures, all zero orphans — recorded in the BUGS row per CLAUDE.md's "not yet possible" rule, along with the two remaining root causes (per-creation-context body duplication for ambiguous closures; env-graph resolution of the Metro global function's `CreateClosure` env operand). Gate green: typecheck + `test:gate` 2276 tests, 2269 pass, 0 fail, 7 skipped. · ~50 tool calls, ~120k tokens

2026-09-05 · Claude Opus 5 (lean worker) · cfg/env-graph: resolve the `CreateClosure` env operand (docs/BUGS.md 2026-09-04 cause (b), 4,009 orphans on react-navigation-example) · Root cause, and it is not a join: `rEnv` never "merged to UNKNOWN" — it was loaded by `LoadConstUndefined`. Hermes ≥ v96 compiles a function that captures nothing to `LoadConstUndefined rE; CreateClosure rD, rE, fn`, and the §6.2 lattice had no value for "undefined", so `clobber` turned it into UNKNOWN. Characterised by bucketing the defining instruction of the env register at all 4,613 unresolved `Create*Closure` sites on the bundle: 1,455 `LoadConstUndefined` in the same block, 2,831 with no in-block definition (the same `LoadConstUndefined` register flowing across a branch — e.g. global fn#0 `12b LoadConstUndefined r6` feeding `1e4/1ef/209/214 CreateClosure rX, r6, fn` two blocks later), and 327 with a real `Create*Environment` definition (those are the 178 `W_AMBIGUOUS_CLOSURE_ENV` of cause (a)). The consequence was the cascade: a no-env function had no `closureEnvOf` entry, so its `selfEnv` was `undefined`, so its `CreateFunctionEnvironment` got no parent and every closure it created was an orphan too (the 1,755). Reproduced from source with hermesc v99 in one line (`var nocap = function (p) { return p * 2; }` inside a capturing function → `[@30] LoadConstUndefined 1; [@32] CreateClosure 3, 1, 3`), so no new fixture was needed — 15 existing construct fixtures already carry the shape at v96/v98/v99. Fix (`src/cfg/env-graph.ts`): a fourth lattice value `none`; `LoadConstUndefined` → `none`; `Create*Closure`/`Create*Class` off a `none` register records `closureEnvOf(fn) = null` (known to capture nothing, not missing); `CreateEnvironment`/`CreateInnerEnvironment` with a `none` parent operand gets parent `null`. Soundness: `none` never overrides a real environment — a function created once with an env and once with `none` becomes `W_AMBIGUOUS_CLOSURE_ENV` instead of being bound. Measured react-navigation-example: `W_ORPHAN_FUNCTION` 4,009 → **0**, known-empty environments 179 → 4,188, resolved environments unchanged at 11,363, unresolved env accesses unchanged at 182; emitted bytes IDENTICAL (`--passes=none` 22,784,763 before and after), so isolated/unbound stay 103/158 passes-on and 106/160 `--passes=none` — **the residual 158 is entirely cause (a)**, which the BUGS row now says. Service NSW `--passes=none` on deb (`~/hbc2js-envgraph`, node 22, nice 10): 78,263,633 bytes, 0 isolated, 0 unbound, 0 orphans, byte-identical before and after; NSW never had the shape (0 missing envs either way). Skipped the 24-min NSW passes-on run — the change does not touch passes and `--passes=none` is byte-identical. Tests: new `tests/gate/cfg/env-no-capture.test.ts` (3 cases — 15 construct fixtures must report no `W_ORPHAN_FUNCTION`; a corpus guard that hermesc still emits the shape; a synthesised two-site module for the soundness half), verified to FAIL 2/3 with the fix stashed and pass with it; new second case in `tests/sweep/emit/unbound-env-slots.test.ts` ratcheting `W_ORPHAN_FUNCTION` ≤ 0 and known-empty ≥ 4,000 on the real bundle (36 s, passing). Docs: `docs/specs/03-cfg.md` §6.2 lattice + transfer rules, `docs/BUGS.md` row (cause (b) marked fixed with numbers; cause (a) now owns the whole residual). Gate green: typecheck clean, `test:gate` 2298 tests, 2291 pass, 0 fail, 7 skipped. · ~33 tool calls, ~95k tokens

2026-09-04 · Claude Opus 5 (lean worker) · fuzz family F2: root-cause + fix the let-capture branch divergence (adversarial fixture 46, DIVERGENT at v96) · Root cause was NOT the env/closure graph: the `expr-rebuild` pass folded a dead store into a `for` loop's *test*, because `topLevelExprOf` names only `test` (the writable field) so a `for` header's `init`/`update` were invisible to `classifySite`, `branchVerdict` and `loopTestGuard`. hermesc drops the provably-false `0 === (outer + '')` and leaves a dead `AddEmptyString r1, r1` in front of the loop's `LoadConstUInt8 r1, 2`; the fold turned `r4 < 2` into `0 < "0"` and the loop never ran. Gate 2298 tests, 1 known load-order flake (`tests/gate/cfg/env-no-capture.test.ts`, passes alone). ~125k tokens.

2026-09-05 · Claude Opus 5 (lean worker) · report §5 "Landing item 2": recursion-group hosting for closure copies · `src/emit/index.ts` only. A duplicated function's copy is hosted in the owner of the environment it captured; when that owner is the duplicated function itself, or another duplicated function that creates it, the host is multi-instance and the copy was emitted inside just one of those instances — so every other instance referenced a body it could not see. The emitter now computes **recursion groups** (Tarjan SCCs over `closureCreationSites` inverted by creating function, restricted to functions with `closureCopies`, self-loop = group of one) and emits a copy hosted inside its own group in *every* instance of that host, under that instance's composed remap. Termination is a `hosted` set threaded down `CopyCtx` (the group-copy names an enclosing instance already hoisted, plus the instance's own name): the copy that would nest inside itself finds its own function declaration, which is the right binding since that site is the self-reference. Same set seeds the travel loop's `travelled`, killing a duplicate declaration at the same level. Replaces the old `selfHosted`/`inCopySubtree` rule (which fired only for the copy-0 instance and only outside any other copy, i.e. for none of this bundle's five groups). react-navigation-example-0.85.3 (strictEnv:false): isolated 32 -> **16**, unbound names 63 -> **28**, and the whole 35-name `_fn<n>__c<i>` family (`_fn12406`/`_fn12407` mutual, `_fn13523`/`_fn15373`/`_fn13078`/`_fn11751` self) went to **0**; bytes 17,923,866 -> 20,240,933 (+13%, one inner-copy body per host instance). Control: rn-template-0.72 (0 ambiguous) `cmp`-identical, 5,000,434 bytes at CLI defaults, against the same tree with only `src/emit/index.ts` reverted (both sides run in a detached worktree at 83f1863 so the other agents' uncommitted edits sit on both). 2026-09-04 · Claude Opus 5 (lean worker) · emit: map statements inside inline function expressions (spec 05 §16.2 gap) · The inline `func` *expression* form printed its body into a separate array spliced into the middle of an enclosing `out` entry, so every statement in it was unmapped (`useEffect(function(){…})`, IIFEs, and — the big one — a generator/async function's whole resume-dispatcher state machine). Design: a **stripped sentinel**. While a hook is live the inline `func` case collects its body's marks against its own array, converts them to lines relative to its own text (header = line 1), pushes one record, and returns its text prefixed with `\uE000<id base36>\uE001` — a private-use marker with no newline. `printProgram`'s existing prefix-sum post-pass finds each sentinel in the same single walk, reads the absolute line it sits on, rebases that record's rows, and REMOVES it, so the returned text stays byte-identical with and without the hook (the corpus-wide assertion is kept). Nested inline functions compose with no recursion: a child's sentinel travels up inside its parent's text and is resolved by the same top-level scan. Rows keep the origin's own `fn` (the INNER function's Hermes index); a record whose sentinel is seen twice or never is dropped, not guessed. Zero cost with no hook — that path is byte-for-byte the pre-sentinel code. `onFunctionRange` fell out of the same record: an inline function expression now reports its own range when it has the `_fnN` name `ranges.jsonl` keys on (anonymous ones still get none), plus any nested `function` declarations. Measured before → after (rawFrames, v96): whole construct corpus 0.3832 → **0.5481** (+3496 rows), `23-generator-basic` 0.1306 → **0.4650**, `27-async-await-basic` 0.1214 → **0.5658**, rn-template whole bundle 0.6408 → **0.6432**; rn-template first-600 (the gate sample) and `02-while-loop`/`04-for-loop-basic` unchanged — at one-function-per-frame granularity most RN closures are hoisted `function _fnN` siblings, already mapped. Floors raised: RN 0.60 → 0.61, while 0.65 → 0.66, plus two new ratchets CORPUS 0.53 and GENERATOR 0.44. Tests (+5 gate, all in `tests/gate/emit/line-map.test.ts`): three rung-private hand-built ASTs (a statement inside an inline function gets a row with the inner `fn`; nested inline functions map to the innermost `fn` with exact line arithmetic; `onFunctionRange` covers the inline function and the declaration inside it) + the two new coverage ratchets. `docs/test-count-baseline.json` 993 → 998. Docs: spec 05 §16.2 rewritten (gap → mechanism), §16.4 numbers and floors, §16's honest-partial list; `src/emit/print.ts`'s `onFunctionRange` doc. docs/UI.md needed no change — its `rowForLine` already drops foreign-`fn` rows. UI follow-up recommended, NOT done (src/artifact is another agent's): `ArtifactService.lineMap` should KEEP the child-`fn` rows (they are true, and the UI could use them to switch the disasm pane to the child listing); today `rowForLine` silently ignores them. `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run test:gate` 2399 tests / 2392 pass / 0 fail / 7 skip, no flakes, no golden or `expected.txt` moved. - 2026-09-04 · Opus · **global-access §4 condition 6 — `pre-guard-clobber` (follow-up to 5846d92 / BUGS T14).** Same root cause as the loop bug (`isProvenGlobal` is position-blind), the other region: condition 3 scans only `L[i+1..j-1]`, so a write between the `globalThis` store and the **guard** was seen by nothing — `[r1 = globalThis; r1 = other; guard(r1,"p"); r0 = r1.p]` folded to bare `p` though `r1` is `other` at the read. Fix: `hasPreGuardClobber` (match.ts) walks `L[0..i-1]` plus every enclosing list's statements before the one containing `L`, outward to `fnBody`, at any nesting depth; the last write found must be valued `globalThis`, else refuse. Added to `classifySite` and re-derived in check.ts item 4c; `isProvenGlobal` unchanged. **End-to-end verdict: NOT synthetic-only.** `14-nested-try-catch/*.obf` (all five versions) is real Hermes output where the `globalThis` register `r3` is reused for `r3 = r3.Error` before two later guards on `r3`, and the pre-fix rung folded both reads; it is harmless there only because the clobbering write sits on an always-throwing path, so the match.ts comment "Hermes's allocator does not reuse a live register" is not a guarantee the rung can lean on. Two limits measured over the whole 768-output construct corpus, not argued: no-visible-pre-guard-write is deliberately NOT a refusal (control-flow-flattened `.obf` puts `rN = globalThis` in another `__pc` dispatch case; refusing there costs 141 outputs their folds for no soundness gain), and the walk is not path-sensitive (cost: the two `14-nested-try-catch/*.obf` guards, readability only). +6 `test(` sites (10 runtime tests): AST + `node:vm` divergence proof in adversarial-ladder §5b, nested-`if`/enclosing-list clobbers, positives for a nested `globalThis`-valued pre-guard write and the documented no-write limit, and the `14-nested-try-catch` `.obf` end-to-end test at all five versions. - 2026-09-04 · Sonnet 5 (lean worker) · **e2e fix: "back/forward restore prior selections" — stale group-opening loop vs the virtualised left tree.** Diagnosis (b), not a flake: reproduced deterministically 3/3 direct runs (`npx playwright test -g "back/forward"`), same failure every time (`locator('[data-group]').nth(2)` timeout, not a random index), despite a loaded box (`uptime` load avg 13–25). Root cause: the group-opening loop (added in `32c71f3`, before `ui/src/panes/LeftPane.tsx` was virtualised with `@tanstack/react-virtual` in `9400d5d`) counted `[data-group]` once and indexed into it; the virtualizer only mounts rows within the current scroll window, so clicking to open group 0 (many module children) pushes groups 1+ out of the rendered window and `nth(2)` never appears — hangs until the 30s test timeout. Fixed by re-querying `[data-group]` after every click and stopping as soon as two modules are visible (`ui/e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), rather than assuming every group is simultaneously mounted. Each row now carries `matched` (members satisfying `--key`/`--value`; the table's own member count when neither is given), a filtered query ranks by `matched` → hit density (`matched / members`) → size (unfiltered still sorts by size alone), and `--min-matched N` (default 1, `?minMatched=`) drops the accidental hit. - 2026-09-04 · Opus 5 (lean worker) · **scoped single-function readable decompile (QUEUE candidate #2, hunt-tooling-backlog #3, docs/DECISIONS.md D25).** `hbc2js decompile <bundle.hbc> --fn N` (+ `hbc2js <bundle.hbc> --fn N`) and library `decompileFunction(bytes, N, opts)`. Mechanism: one new `EmitOptions.onlyFunction` on the SAME `emitModule` (no pipeline fork) — reconstructs the exact `hosted` set N's primary emission receives by walking N's ancestor chain (O(chain depth), no sibling emitted), then `emitOne(N)` renders N + its closure subtree byte-identically to N's slice of the full render (children hoisted/inlined identically, same names), modulo top-level indentation; scope-check is skipped and a header comment marks the fragment (captured outer-scope idents appear free). `decompileFunction` = `decompile(bytes,{emit:{onlyFunction:N}})`, so all opts/timings/diagnostics behave identically. CLI: `--fn N`/`--fn=N` + a `decompile` verb alias; USAGE updated (help gate is loose /Usage:/). Measured on NSW (43,384 fns, numbers only in D25): scoped `decompileFunction` 3.4 s of which only 25 ms is N's own structure+stageA+stageB — the rest is the inherently-global parse(56ms)+analyse(886ms)+env-graph(~1.7s)+placement(~0.7s), which is NOT scoped away (closure ownership + placement span the whole module); whole-bundle render is minutes (prior ~90s dist figure; under this session's node type-strip loader it had not finished after 20 min) → >25× win, tens of ms incremental per function. Env graph / placement being global is the honest residue, recorded in D25. Skipped MCP/ui-server routes (artifact `source(fn)` already serves pre-rendered per-function text; a live scoped verb was not trivially additive) — no spec-17 change. Tests: `tests/gate/emit/scoped-decompile.test.ts` (+2: identity scoped-node deep-equals whole-render node across fixtures/versions incl. nested closures + captured vars, programmatic not literal; complexity via injected astPasses counter proving scoped touches fewer functions), `tests/gate/cli/cli.test.ts` (+1: CLI `--fn` end-to-end + bad-index E_USAGE). `docs/test-count-baseline.json` 1096 → 1099 (+3 own, re-derived from committed HEAD). `npm run typecheck` clean; gate green. - 2026-09-05 | Claude Sonnet 5 (lean worker) | CI red-run fix: 3 gate tests failing on GitHub only (gh run 33920756693, ubuntu/macos-latest node 22.18) | FIXED (6c1a300). Root cause 1/2 (`tests/gate/docs/ci-require-oracles-skip.test.ts`, tests 395/396): both assertions matched the spec reporter's `ℹ skipped`/`ℹ fail` lines from a spawned child `node --test`; node 22.18's default reporter for a piped (non-TTY) child is `tap` ("# skipped 1", no ℹ), node 24/25's is `spec` even when piped — always green on this Mac, always red on CI's node-22 job. Three levels over the SAME fetched neighbourhood, none of which fetches anything new: `far` folds functions into `mod:<n>` bundles with weighted edges (`bundleByModule`; a function of unknown module is never guessed into someone else's box), `mid` is the existing function neighbourhood, `near` opens the focus into a card (`lodCard`, capped at 8 with "+N more") that says `blocks: CFG pending (spec 26 L9)` - `FocusCard` in `ui/src/graph/nodes.tsx` is the exact slot L9 replaces. `lodLevel(zoom, prev)` is pure and hysteretic (0.5 / 1.6, 12% sticky band) and only a USER gesture moves the level (React Flow reports a null source event for its own `fitView`/Controls moves, and a neighbourhood fitted into the 280 px panel already sits below 0.5 zoom - deriving the level from it would have re-drawn the pane under the analyst). Level changes expand/collapse through `expandGraphNode`/`collapseGraphNode`, bounded by `LOD_EXPAND_CAP = 8`; `mid`/`near` never auto-expand (auto-expanding on zoom would make the node set a function of the viewport and would have broken e2e tests 2/3/8). Reset returns to `rootLod`. Also registered `graph.followToggle` (`g f`) and `graph.lodCycle` (`g z`) in the SHARED registry + all three presets (the previous graph agent could not touch that file) with two new no-op-able `ActionApi` methods; `view.graph` stays gated off, no assertion inverted.
